What brings you in today?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 20 / therapist / high_school\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Diminished_Thinking\nmanifestation: \"I'm here because lately I can't keep my head clear at work—I lose track of what clients are saying, reread the same chart note over and over, and then freeze on simple decisions I used to handle.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Pleasing patient.\nA pleasing patient may\n1) minimize, downplay, or describe in more favorable terms symptoms and behaviors that they believe could create a negative impression,\n2) initially withhold or delay disclosing sensitive clinical information due to shame or fear of being judged, and\n3) seek confirmation that their descriptions or answers are appropriate and demonstrate a tendency to seek the doctor's approval.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. I guess that’s around when I started noticing things getting harder.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless or overly guilty lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I wouldn’t want to overstate it, but lately I do get pretty hard on myself. Like if I miss one detail in a note, or I don’t return a call right away, I’ll keep thinking I’m useless or that I’m letting the clients down, and everyone at the clinic too. I know that probably sounds dramatic, but it feels really hard to shake once it starts.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m… not totally sure how to answer that. I wouldn’t say I’m sad all day, every day, exactly. I mean, I’ve been more down than usual, yeah, but “most of the day nearly every day” sounds pretty strong. Maybe I’m just trying not to make it sound worse than it is.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually tired or low on energy lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have. I keep telling myself it’s just being busy, but even small stuff feels heavier than it should. Like showering, or driving to the clinic, or trying to finish paperwork… it feels like I’m dragging around a backpack full of bricks. I don’t know if that’s the right way to put it, but yeah, my energy’s been pretty low.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your appetite lately?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah. I don’t think it’s anything extreme, but most days I kind of… forget to eat unless someone points it out or reminds me. I’m just not really hungry. And my pants have been fitting looser, so I guess I’ve been skipping meals more than I realized. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts that life isn’t worth living, or thoughts of hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: I… yeah, kind of. I don’t want to worry anyone, and I don’t have a plan to hurt myself or anything like that. But I do keep having thoughts like… it would be easier if I just didn’t wake up. And that scares me, because I know that’s not nothing. Is that… is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have these symptoms been going on for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. It’s probably been about a month now, maybe a little more. It started around when the clinic lost some staff and my schedule shifted into more late-evening coverage, plus a lot more crisis paperwork. I kept assuming I was just adjusting, or being dramatic about it, but… it hasn’t really let up. Is that the kind of timeframe you mean?\n\nDoctor: During the past two weeks, have you felt down or depressed nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m… not totally sure how to measure “nearly every day.” I mean, I’ve had a lot of low days, yeah, but I don’t want to overstate it if that makes sense. Some days I can still kind of get through things and act normal, so I’m not sure if that counts as depressed or just… off.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Hi… yeah, thanks for seeing me. I guess I’m here because my head just hasn’t felt very clear lately, especially at work. It’s probably not as bad as I’m making it sound, but I keep losing track of what clients are saying, and I’ll reread the same chart note over and over like it’s not going in. Then even simple decisions I used to make pretty easily, I kind of freeze on. I’m not sure if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: I mean… maybe a little? I wouldn’t say I’m, like, depressed-depressed, if that makes sense. Just kind of low sometimes, or not quite myself. But I don’t want to overstate it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been getting less enjoyment from things you usually like to do?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have, a bit. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but my guitar’s just been sitting in the corner, and I keep telling people I’ll come to soccer or go help at the shelter, and then I bail. Even stuff I normally like, like cooking or going hiking, I can do it, but it kind of feels like I’m just going through the motions. Like there’s not much point to it. Does that… count?\n\nDoctor: How has your sleep been lately?\n\nPatient: It’s… not great, I guess. I mean, I do get tired, so I keep thinking I’ll just crash when I get home, but then I’m lying there for hours kind of replaying the whole day in my head. And if I do fall asleep, I wake up before my alarm and feel like I barely slept. It’s probably pretty normal sometimes, but… yeah, it’s been happening enough that I notice it.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying a lot?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t know. Maybe a normal amount? I mean, I think about stuff, like anyone does, but I’m not sure I’d call it “worrying a lot.” What do you mean by that exactly?\n\nDoctor: Before the last few months, did you have problems with attention or focus when you were a child?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not really. I mean, I wasn’t perfect or anything, but I don’t remember teachers or my parents saying I couldn’t focus, and I got through school okay. So I’d say no, unless there’s something specific you mean by that?\n\nDoctor: Did something particularly stressful happen in your life before these problems started?\n\nPatient: I mean… yeah, kind of. I don’t want to make it sound like it was some huge thing, but about a month ago the clinic lost some staff, and my schedule got shifted into more late-evening coverage. There’s also been more crisis paperwork than usual. I guess that’s around when I started noticing things getting harder.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless or overly guilty lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I wouldn’t want to overstate it, but lately I do get pretty hard on myself. Like if I miss one detail in a note, or I don’t return a call right away, I’ll keep thinking I’m useless or that I’m letting the clients down, and everyone at the clinic too. I know that probably sounds dramatic, but it feels really hard to shake once it starts.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m… not totally sure how to answer that. I wouldn’t say I’m sad all day, every day, exactly. I mean, I’ve been more down than usual, yeah, but “most of the day nearly every day” sounds pretty strong. Maybe I’m just trying not to make it sound worse than it is.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually tired or low on energy lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have. I keep telling myself it’s just being busy, but even small stuff feels heavier than it should. It’s probably been about a month now, maybe a little more. It started around when the clinic lost some staff and my schedule shifted into more late-evening coverage, plus a lot more crisis paperwork. I kept assuming I was just adjusting, or being dramatic about it, but… it hasn’t really let up. Is that the kind of timeframe you mean?\n\nDoctor: During the past two weeks, have you felt down or depressed nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m… not totally sure how to measure “nearly every day.” I mean, I’ve had a lot of low days, yeah, but I don’t want to overstate it if that makes sense. Some days I can still kind of get through things and act normal, so I’m not sure if that counts as depressed or just… off.\n\nDoctor: Over the past month, have you been feeling excessively worried or anxious on most days?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it “excessively,” exactly. I guess I’d need to know what you mean by anxious on most days… I don’t want to answer it wrong.\n\nDoctor: In the past two weeks, have you felt down or depressed on most days?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not completely sure. So I’d say no, unless there’s something specific you mean by that?\n\nDoctor: Did something particularly stressful happen in your life before these problems started?\n\nPatient: I mean… yeah, kind of. I don’t want to make it sound like it was some huge thing, but about a month ago the clinic lost some staff, and my schedule got shifted into more late-evening coverage. There’s also been more crisis paperwork than usual. I guess that’s around when I started noticing things getting harder.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless or overly guilty lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I wouldn’t want to overstate it, but lately I do get pretty hard on myself. Like if I miss one detail in a note, or I don’t return a call right away, I’ll keep thinking I’m useless or that I’m letting the clients down, and everyone at the clinic too. I know that probably sounds dramatic, but it feels really hard to shake once it starts.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m… not totally sure how to answer that. I wouldn’t say I’m sad all day, every day, exactly. I mean, I’ve been more down than usual, yeah, but “most of the day nearly every day” sounds pretty strong. Maybe I’m just trying not to make it sound worse than it is.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually tired or low on energy lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have. I keep telling myself it’s just being busy, but even small stuff feels heavier than it should. Like showering, or driving to the clinic, or trying to finish paperwork… it feels like I’m dragging around a backpack full of bricks. I don’t know if that’s the right way to put it, but yeah, my energy’s been pretty low.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your appetite lately?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah. I don’t think it’s anything extreme, but most days I kind of… forget to eat unless someone points it out or reminds me. I’m just not really hungry. And my pants have been fitting looser, so I guess I’ve been skipping meals more than I realized. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts that life isn’t worth living, or thoughts of hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: I… yeah, kind of. I don’t want to worry anyone, and I don’t have a plan to hurt myself or anything like that. But I do keep having thoughts like… it would be easier if I just didn’t wake up. And that scares me, because I know that’s not nothing. Is that… is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have these symptoms been going on for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. It’s probably been about a month now, maybe a little more. It started around when the clinic lost some staff and my schedule shifted into more late-evening coverage, plus a lot more crisis paperwork. I kept assuming I was just adjusting, or being dramatic about it, but… it hasn’t really let up. Is that the kind of timeframe you mean?\n\nDoctor: During the past two weeks, have you felt down or depressed nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m… not totally sure how to measure “nearly every day.” I mean, I’ve had a lot of low days, yeah, but I don’t want to overstate it if that makes sense. Some days I can still kind of get through things and act normal, so I’m not sure if that counts as depressed or just… off.\n\nDoctor: Over the past month, have you been feeling excessively worried or anxious on most days?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it “excessively,” exactly. I mean, I worry about things, sure, but I’m not sure it’s more than what most people would have going on. I guess I’d need to know what you mean by anxious on most days… I don’t want to answer it wrong.\n\nDoctor: In the past two weeks, have you felt down or depressed on most days?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not completely sure. I mean, there have definitely been days where I’ve felt pretty low, but “most days” feels hard to say. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is if it’s not. Maybe… some days more than others? Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you felt down or depressed on more days than not?\n\nPatient: I think… maybe, yeah? I’m still not totally confident saying it that way, because some days are more like I’m just getting by rather than fully “depressed.” But if you mean feeling low or down at least part of the day, then probably more days than not. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do these symptoms feel mainly caused by the changes and stress at work?\n\nPatient: I think that’s probably a big part of it, yeah. It’s been about a month, since the clinic lost some staff and my schedule got shifted into late-evening coverage, plus more of the crisis paperwork. I don’t want to overstate it, but the timing does seem to line up with that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling tense or on edge a lot lately?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not sure. Maybe a little sometimes, but I don’t know if I’d say “a lot.” I haven’t really been tracking that specifically. What do you mean by tense or on edge—like physically, or more mentally?\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you felt sad or empty for most of the day on more days than not?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say most of the day, exactly. There are definitely stretches where I feel kind of low or flat, but I’m not sure I’ve been paying close enough attention to say it’s most of the day on more days than not. Is it okay to answer it that way?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
